  `Config`'s mapping is unit-tested on a map of variables,
  `ConfigService::with_vars`, which never touches `std::env`.
- **oauth2 5.0.0 (2025-01-21) is past the bar, and its pin says until when.**
  oauth2 now lends only RFC 6749's types and flow; without a release by
  2027-01-31 it is replaced. poem, which shared this bullet, left every lockfile
  (*poem leaves the public surface and the dependency tree*), and multer,
  serde_urlencoded and heck joined the record (*Dependencies*).
- **RUSTSEC-2026-0253 (`lru` 0.16, `LruCache::pop` unsound when a key's `Drop`
  panics) is accepted in `deny.toml` with its reason:** async-graphql 7.2.1
  reaches `pop` only through its dataloader `LruCache` factory and the Apollo
  persisted-queries store, and nestrs builds neither — `#[dataloader]` is
  `DataLoader::new`, which does not cache. The fix ships with async-graphql 8, still a
  release candidate on 2026-10-27 (8.0.0-rc.5), so 7.0 keeps 7.2.1 and the ignore.

### The rules are held by types, lints and behaviour tests

For a contributor. Through 7.0's development `nest-rs-conformance` grew into a
`syn`-based scanner of the framework's own source — "joins" proving a rule was
followed, and a `blinds` join proving the others could not be evaded — and three
audit rounds kept finding constructions that hid a member from a join. None of
the 27 caught a production defect after it landed. A source scanner sees
spellings while the compiler resolves items, so a rule is now held by the first
rung that can hold it (`CLAUDE.md`, *How a rule is held*; the history is
`.claude/decisions/conformance-scanner.md`):

- **Types.** `Transport::stop_bound` is required, `DurationBounds` and `Unit` are
  built only through constructors that check them at compile time, the decorator
  pairs are `nest_rs_codegen::pair::ALL`, every `key = value` decorator reads
  through `Grammar`, and `nest-rs-queue`'s capability test is an exhaustive
  `match`, so a new `Capability` does not compile until its refusal is tested.
- **rustc and clippy.** One `clippy.toml` at the repository root covers
  `crates/`, `demo/` and `bench/`: `tokio::main` is a disallowed macro, and
  `std::env::var` and `var_os` are disallowed methods. Both
  workspaces deny `unwrap_used`, `expect_used`, `panic`, `print_stdout`,
  `print_stderr`, `map_err_ignore`, `let_underscore_must_use`,
  `allow_attributes_without_reason`, `dbg_macro`, `todo`, `unimplemented`,
  `wildcard_imports` and `self_named_module_files`, with `allow-*-in-tests` in
  `clippy.toml` and one `#![allow]` per suite root. An exception is
  `#[expect(lint, reason = "…")]` at the site, so a stale one fails the build.
  `unsafe_code` is `deny` rather than `forbid` in the root workspace, so the three
  crates that had opted out of the whole table opt in. The sweep found the five
  defects fixed above.
- **Behaviour tests** — a
  unit test over the Redis key constants, the doctor's differential test in `nest-rs-cli`, an MCP
  test that a decode failure behind `anyhow` reaches the operator's line without
  its value.
- **Review**, against a written sentence, for what none of these can see.

`nest-rs-conformance` is removed: its 17,875 lines and 106 tests go, and what
they checked is held by the rungs above or by review. The four source-reading
tests left elsewhere are replaced too:

- **`nest-rs-macro-hygiene` holds a real entity**, so `#[expose]` (both arms),
  `#[crud]` with every operation and `#[authorize(Action, Entity)]` at HTTP,
  GraphQL, WS and MCP compile behind the single `nest-rs` dependency — sea-orm's
  derives emit relative `sea_orm::` paths, which `nest_rs::seaorm::sea_orm`
  satisfies. Its scan of every `*-macros` source is gone: the `::uuid` that
  `#[crud]` once emitted now fails this crate's build.
- **A pooled guard runs every check it overrides, whatever marker it declares.**
  The pool dispatches on `dyn Guard`, so a missing marker never opens an edge;
  a test per edge proves it, and the scan for guards overriding a `check_*`
  without their marker is gone. A marker still matters where a decorator binds
  the guard, and is refused there at compile time.
- **`#[mcp]`'s grammar lives in `nest_rs_codegen`** (`MCP_GRAMMAR`,
  `mcp_answers`), and `nest-rs-mcp` destructures `McpIdentity` without `..`
  against it, so a field added without an answer in `#[mcp]` fails the build or
  its test rather than a test reading `identity.rs`.
- **`CronExpression`'s presets are declared through one macro** that also lists
  them for the pinned-instants test; the public constants are unchanged. The
  `#[input]` rustdoc keeps its contract in prose instead of a transcribed
  expansion a test kept in step.
- **`cargo hack --each-feature`** builds the umbrella and
  `nest-rs-macro-hygiene` under each feature alone, and `nest-rs-macro-hygiene`
  has one feature per decorator-owning capability, so each capability's
  decorators are proved to compile under that capability's feature alone.
- **The definition of done is `just ci` green**: the recipes CI runs, in the
  same order.
- **The rules are rewritten** so they agree with each other and each names how
  it is held: `CLAUDE.md`, the zone rules in `.claude/rules/`, and
  `.claude/decisions/`, one file per decision recording what was tried and what
  retired it. Rustdoc cites the zone rule that holds its decision.

### CI is the gate, and the rules fit their budget

For a contributor.

- **CI gates every push and pull request** (`.github/workflows/framework.yml`) with
  the Justfile's own recipes, so it runs exactly what `just ci` runs locally:
  `just lint` (rustfmt; the workflows' syntax and hardening, with actionlint and
  zizmor's offline audits; clippy over every feature, warnings denied; each
  capability of the umbrella alone, with cargo-hack; cargo-deny's bans,
  licences and sources), `just doc` (rustdoc over every feature, private items
  included, warnings denied) and `just test` (every test and doc example,
  against real Postgres, Redis and RustFS; `just test integration` runs the
  in-process suites without them). The advisories are `just audit`, over the
  framework's and the demo's lockfiles, and a change answers only for what it
  brings. On a pull request touching a Cargo tree, `audit.yml` runs `just audit
  <base>`: it refuses an advisory absent at the pull request's base and warns of
  one the base already holds. No workflow audits a branch on a clock: GitHub's
  Dependabot watches the default branch's lockfiles for the vulnerabilities
  GitHub's advisory database holds, its alerts and security updates turned on
  in the repository's settings (version updates are off); an unmaintained
  crate, a yanked version or an unsound one GitHub has not imported, like any
  advisory on a release branch, surfaces at the next pull request touching a
  Cargo tree. `publish.yml` keeps the whole check before publishing, so a
  release ships no advisory nobody decided: its exit is a bump, or an `ignore`
  line in `deny.toml` with its reason. The docs build has its own workflow,
  started by a change to the pages; `demo/` and `bench/` are separate projects
  with their own commands.
- **What CI does not run is not held, so CI runs it.** Every suite runs on
  Valkey 9.1.2, PostgreSQL 18.6 and RustFS 1.0.1, the latest releases, as the
  dev container does; `demo.yml` runs the demo's lint (clippy, rustfmt and the
  tree's own `nestrs lint`) and every suite of it, the queue's e2e among them, on
  a change to its tree, to the framework it builds on (its lockfile included,
  which the CLI that lints the demo is built from), or to the dev container files
  its services start from — the compose files, the Postgres configuration and the
  scripts under `.devcontainer/` — so a Postgres or Valkey image bump, a
  `pg_hba.conf` edit or a change to the certificate script runs the demo's suites
  as it runs the framework's; a change to a docs page, or to a Markdown file under
  a crate's `src/`, starts CI, since the Redis e2e runs the pages' ACL rules as
  printed and the CLI compiles its rules template in. The benchmarks and the beta
  toolchain run on no schedule: the benchmark's SUT is built by hand, and a
  toolchain moves through `rust-toolchain.toml`.
- **cargo-deny is the dependency policy.** `deny.toml` denies known vulnerable,
  unsound, unmaintained and yanked crates, licences outside an allow-list
  (MPL-2.0 accepted crate by crate), sources other than crates.io and `ring`,
  so every TLS client and signer runs on one crypto provider, aws-lc-rs. Each
  ignore names its advisory and its reason, and an ignore or a licence nothing
  needs any more fails the check.
- **The workflows are hardened.** Actions are pinned to commits, checkout
  credentials are dropped, permissions are granted per job, and the deploy and
  release workflows restore no cache.
- **Unused dependencies removed.** `validator` is gone from nest-rs-openapi,
  nest-rs-redis, nest-rs-storage and nest-rs-throttler, `subtle` from
  nest-rs-authn, and `tracing` from nest-rs-oauth-server.
- **docs.rs renders every feature** for nest-rs, nest-rs-authz, nest-rs-redis,
  nest-rs-seaorm and nest-rs-testing, so feature-gated items
  and the links to them appear. Broken intra-doc links in eleven crates are
  fixed.
- **The docs lint is retired.** The site's own build (Astro, Starlight) is the
  docs gate, on every pull request touching `docs/`, and what a page states
  about the code is checked by its author against `STYLE.md` § F, like the rest
  of the prose. The `canon` generator that fed the lint is gone with it. The
  unrendered `tier:` frontmatter is removed.
- **The pages teach what the code asks for.** Response masking, CRUD, OpenAPI
  and the tutorial declared a route's posture through the
  `Authorize<A, S>` parameter, which the hard rules forbid; they now use
  `#[authorize(Action, Entity)]`. Snippets returning an undefined
  `AccessTokenDto` use the framework's
  `nest_rs::oauth::server::AccessTokenResponse`. The file-upload, streaming,
  password, policy-test, e2e-layout, seeding, migrator, claims, social-login,
  guards and dataloader excerpts match the demo again.
- **The rules fit their budget.** CLAUDE.md is a 200-line map and the
  `architecture.md` every scaffold ships as `AGENTS.md` is 250 lines instead
  of 449, with its tables unchanged. A committed Claude Code hook formats every
  Rust file an agent edits, skipping trybuild fixtures, whose snapshots pin
  line numbers.

### Postgres client certificates have a live test

The `postgres-mutual-tls` dev container environment runs a Postgres that lets
one role in on a client certificate alone; `nest-rs-seaorm`'s `mutual_tls`
tests present the dev container's and omit it, run there by hand
(`test.just`'s `postgres` line), since the everyday Postgres and CI's sign in
by password.

### The pipeline's cost, measured

`crates/nest-rs-core/benches/` measures what each request pays the pipeline —
a dispatch of each shape, the ambient installs, a task's capture, a chain's
composition — and the HTTP benchmark gains the tiers a product route runs:
a guarded route, a streamed answer, a `POST` body, the access line, a large
body, `h2c` and a compressed answer.

### The stale-dependency exception is closed

- **The stale-dependency exception is closed**: poem, past the twelve-month
  freshness bar since its last release (3.1.12, 2025-07-28), leaves every
  lockfile, and with it its pin's dated condition and the dependency policy's
  ban. The two decoders taken in its place, multer (3.1.0, 2024-05-04) and
  serde_urlencoded (0.7.1, 2022-01-17), are finished crates without an
  advisory, flagged at their pins, and the ones axum and async-graphql read
  with.

### Also

- **Fixed: `nest-rs-redis`'s e2e suite no longer files keys on development's
  database.** Every test without a database of its own ran on 0, the one
  `nestrs run dev` drains, and left its queue streams there — and, on a failure, its
  keys. Every Valkey of the dev container now declares thirty-two databases
  (`--databases 32` on the standalone server, `databases` and `cluster-databases` on the
  Sentinel and Cluster nodes), `test.just` exports the suite's URL on 16, the shared
  tests' own, and the suite refuses a URL naming another. A Valkey already running keeps
  the count it started with until it is recreated from the compose files.
- **Added: `nest_rs::testing::url_on`, `url_as` and `url_at` put the suites'
  URL on a test's own database, as its own user, or at a proxy's address.**
  Each replaces one RFC 3986 component — the path, the userinfo, the host and
  port — and keeps the scheme, the credentials, the query and the fragment the
  deployment set; a user and a password are percent-encoded.
  `EphemeralDatabase` builds its database's URL with `url_on`, and the
  framework's proxies in front of the suites' Redis and S3 hand out their URL
  with `url_at`, where each wrote `redis://<address>/` and dropped the
  credentials and the query the suite ran with.
- **The development services speak TLS alone, and no certificate is
  committed.** Postgres, Valkey and RustFS present one certificate
  `.devcontainer/scripts/issue-certificates.sh` issues at setup, under an authority that joins
  the system's trust store in the dev container and on the CI runner; CI starts
  them from the dev container's own `docker-compose.yml`. Every suite runs
  encrypted, its doubles included, which end TLS on both sides with a
  certificate of a `TestAuthority`'s; the certificates the suites committed are
  gone. A test proxy that parsed a `MONITOR` line now reads an IPv6 client's
  address whole.
- **Added: `nest_rs::testing::wait_until` and `wait_for` wait on what a test
  cannot await** — progress another task makes, a row a worker writes — polling
  until a condition holds and failing the test at the caller's line once its
  bound elapses, rather than hanging or passing the wait to a later assertion
  that names something else. On a paused clock the wait costs nothing. The
  framework's suites and the queue kit wait through them.
- **Fixed: Rust 1.100 builds every `#[messages]` gateway and `#[process]`
  method without a warning.** 1.100 (2026-11-12) makes `std::convert::Infallible`
  the never type, and both expansions handed a handler's error to a call —
  `ErrorReport(err)`, `JobError::retry(err)` — which, for an error with no value,
  is an unreachable call: every gateway warned, through the reply probe's own
  `Infallible`, and so did a processor returning `Result<(), Infallible>`. The
  error now reaches its report in library code, and `nest-rs-macro-hygiene`
  compiles a handler failing with `!` at every edge that accepts one, so the
  pinned toolchain refuses the next expansion that does it. **Breaking for code
  naming `nest_rs::ws::ReplyOutcome`:** `Failed` carries the `ErrorReport<E>`.
- **Fixed: the boot refuses a Redis budget a port would give up before, and a
  queue lease a renewal cannot fit in.** `<PREFIX>_REDIS__CONNECT_TIMEOUT_SECS`
  accepted up to an hour beside the queue port's and the rate limiter's 18-second
  nets, which then gave up on a command still answering and replaced its cause
  with a bare timeout; and a lease of 10 s under the default 10 s budget could
  lapse while its renewal was still waiting on Redis. Importing
  `RedisQueueModule` or `RedisThrottlerModule` now fails the boot on a budget at
  or past the port's net (`nest_rs::core::BudgetPastNetError`), and the queue
  binding on a lease of no more than one and a half budgets
  (`RedisError::BudgetPastLease`): a renewal is sent a third into the lease and
  may wait out the whole budget.
  `RedisScheduleModule` refuses a budget at or past the scheduler's net,
  `nest_rs::schedule::LOCK_TIMEOUT` — 50 seconds, the wait a claim made on time
  gets at the shortest hold — past which the scheduler abandoned a claim Redis
  was still answering and skipped its occurrence without the cause. The defaults
  — a 10 s budget, a 30 s lease — pass.
- **Fixed: a renewal the backend does not answer holds no lease past its end.**
  The worker checked for a lapsed lease only between renewals, so one renewal
  hanging on a silent backend kept every attempt it covered running until the
  port's 18-second net gave up on it — long after the backend let another
  delivery take the job. Each lease now lapses on time while the renewal waits,
  and its attempt is cut as one no renewal confirmed.
- **Breaking: no line and no span names the client's address.** An address is
  personal data, and a line or a span is kept wherever the deployment ships it.
  The HTTP access line drops `client_ip` and `forwarded`, the request span drops
  `client.address` (OpenTelemetry recommends it; nothing requires it), the
  `rate limit exceeded` warning on HTTP drops `client` and keeps the route, and
  the warning that forwarding headers disagree names the proxy that sent them,
  not the addresses they claim. On WebSockets the same warning's `client` was
  the connection's number, which this process minted: it is kept, as
  `connection`. The address still keys the rate-limit bucket — through a
  pseudonym wherever the counters leave the process, below — and still reaches a
  handler through `ClientIp`; a dashboard filtering on `client_ip` reads the
  ingress's own log instead.
- **Breaking: no rate-limit store outside the process holds a client.** A bucket
  is keyed by its subject — the route and the client's address on HTTP, the
  field and the actor on GraphQL and MCP — and `RedisThrottler` kept that subject
  as its key, where a replica, a backup or a `SCAN` read every client's address,
  and named it in its warning when Redis failed. A store whose counters leave the
  process (`ThrottlerStore::in_process`, `false` unless a store says otherwise;
  `InMemoryThrottler` says `true`) is now handed the first 128 bits of an
  HMAC-SHA256 of the subject, in hex, keyed by `<PREFIX>_THROTTLER__PSEUDONYM_KEY`
  (`ThrottlerConfig::pseudonym_key`: 32 bytes or more, the same on every replica,
  never shown by `Debug`). Beside such a store, `ThrottlerModule::for_root`
  refuses to boot without it, naming the store and the variable, since replicas
  keyed apart would each count their own buckets. `ThrottlerGuard::new` is no
  longer public: `for_root` builds the guard, and a guard the container builds by
  any other path fails the boot rather than count in a bare store.
- **The Redis queue costs less where it used to grow.** Measured on Redis 8.6.3,
  before and after, interleaved (medians of 3 to 5 runs): a worker's look for
  lapsed leases beside 100 000 deliveries running elsewhere cost Redis 16.1 ms
  of CPU a second, now 7.4 (`XPENDING` 12 ms → 0.3 ms) — a pending list of up
  to ten thousand entries is still filtered whole by Redis, and a longer one is
  read a page of a thousand per look. A method
  declaring `retries` no longer copies the stored job for every attempt another
  may follow: 34 → 28 µs of worker CPU per job carrying 1 000 numbers, what the
  same method without retries costs. The producer loads its scripts at boot and
  spells a queue's keys once (16 concurrent pushers: 128 000 → 142 000 pushes a
  second), and every socket sets `TCP_NODELAY` (within the noise on loopback,
  where no acknowledgement is delayed). Drains, latency and the idle cost are
  unchanged.
- **What holds each rule 7.0 states**, now that the source scanner is gone (see
  *The rules are held by types, lints and behaviour tests* above): the `nestrs:`
  key constants obey the key law — each a level of its owner's concern, a
  queue's keys naming the queue first, none a twin of another or a prefix inside
  a level (a unit test over `nest-rs-redis`'s key constants); every queue
  `Capability` is refused where a backend lacks it (an exhaustive `match` in
  `nest-rs-queue`'s tests, so a new variant does not compile until it is). A key
  a page, a chart or a script spells, a compile-fail fixture's snapshot, the stem
  every type in a `module.rs` shares, where an edge folder sits, and every
  `nest_rs…::` path a page names are review items, as are every error type
  living in `error.rs`, a root file of an adapter crate serving more than one
  binding, `Config` naming a `#[config]` alone and a file under an edge folder
  serving that edge alone.
- **`rsa` leaves every application's tree.** `jsonwebtoken` signs and verifies
  on aws-lc-rs, which rustls already brings, rather than on its pure-Rust
  backend, whose `rsa` carries RUSTSEC-2023-0071 (the Marvin timing attack) with
  no fixed release. nestrs never held an RSA private key — its tokens are HMAC or
  EdDSA — but the crate was in the tree of every app depending on `authn`.
- **Fixed: a decorator's compile error points at the code it refuses.** Since
  2.0 every expansion is re-rooted at `::nest_rs::`, and the tokens that rewrite
  added carried the attribute's span, so an error on a rewritten path — an
  operation's return type a `#[operations]` or `#[tools]` refuses — pointed at
  the attribute, with "this error originates in the attribute macro", rather
  than at the signature. The rewrite now keeps the span of the path it replaces.
- **Fixed: a module importing `X::for_root(..)` reaches what `X` provides.** The
  access graph read static imports only, so a provider beside a `for_root` import
  that injected what the configured module provides — `Storage` beside
  `StorageModule::for_root(..)` — failed the boot asking for an import already
  written. A dynamic import now imports the module it declares, as a NestJS
  `DynamicModule` names its `module`: every module owning a config, `McpModule`
  and `OAuthResourceModule` alike. **Breaking for a hand-written `DynamicModule`:**
  it gains the required `fn module() -> TypeId`, naming the module its
  `register` wires, or its own type when it wires none and adds global
  infrastructure only. The graph reads the declaration, never what `register`
  happens to wire first.
- **Every doc example is compiled, and run unless it serves forever.** 87 were
  `ignore`, so they tested nothing, and several had drifted from the API they
  showed: `OpenApiModule::for_root()` with no argument, a guard logging on a target
  its crate does not have, `GraphqlModule::for_root()`, a `#[routes]` method with
  no receiver, a `VERSION` constant the macro emits as `VERSIONS`. A macro's
  examples sit on its surface crate's re-export and prove what the expansion
  provides, where an *Expands to* sketch of `#[doc(hidden)]` internals was, and an
  example showing a composition sits with its owner (`#[authorize]` on each edge
  in `nest-rs-authz`, `#[crud]` on `CrudService`). The filters example no longer
  answers a 500 with the error's text.
- **The decorators' refusals are pinned in one suite, through the umbrella.**
  The twelve trybuild suites, one per surface crate and each written through
  that crate's own paths, are one suite in `nest-rs-macro-hygiene`: a folder per
  umbrella module, each fixture written through `nest_rs::` as a developer
  writes it, so a snapshot shows the span and the paths a developer meets —
  which is how the span above was found. One trybuild project also ends the
  suites rebuilding ring and its dependents for each other (cargo#16134), and
  the `unsafe` environment workaround two of them carried for it.
- **`unreachable_pub` is a workspace lint**, and every crate now opts into
  `[lints] workspace = true`, so an item no caller outside its crate reaches is
  `pub(crate)`.
- **Fixed: one control character in a `Server-Timing` `desc` no longer drops the
  whole header**, every other entry and the total with it; it is written as a
  space.
- **Fixed: a remote parent the subscriber cannot take is reported.** When the
  OpenTelemetry layer is missing from a span's subscriber, or the span started
  before the link, every continued trace exported without its parent in silence;
  one `warn` on `nest_rs::opentelemetry`, `remote parent not linked; continued
  traces export without their parent`, now says so, once per process. A span the
  layer's own filter disabled stays silent.
- **Fixed: an OAuth client endpoint URL that does not parse says why** — the
  parser's reason is in the error beside the value.
- **Four TLS tests pass on macOS.** The client trusts the fixture authority alone,
  verified by rustls with webpki on every platform, rather than merging it into the
  platform store, whose server-certificate policy rejected the long-lived fixtures.
- **The documentation gains** [Upgrading from 6.x to 7.0](https://nestrs.dev/upgrading/),
  and in the queue section *Delivery on Redis*, *Concurrency and scaling* and
  *Upgrading queues from 6.x*; the environment reference documents every variable
  above and what the boot says about one nothing reads; and every queue page's
  output is pasted from a 7.0 run. The schedule page no longer says a successful
  tick is silent, or that a tick opens a `scheduled job` span: it files one
  `schedule.tick` line on `nest_rs::operation`, in a span of that name.

- **Breaking:** `nest_rs_codegen::Edge`, the five edges that refuse
  `version = "…"`, is `nest_rs_codegen::VersionedEdge`: one word, one concept,
  now that the kernel's `Edge` names every framework edge.
- **Fixed:** a `main` that panics no longer holds the exit for work it left behind.
  The panic unwound past `#[nest_rs::main]`'s bounded teardown, and dropping the
  runtime waited, with no bound and no line, for every `spawn_blocking` still running —
  a synchronous `Drop`, a stuck file read — until the orchestrator's `SIGKILL`. The
  teardown now gets what the shutdown hooks left of their budget whether `main` returns
  or panics, says at `warn` what it abandoned, and the panic goes on out with its status
  (101) and its one event.
- **A DataLoader batch runs under the trace of the request that triggered it.** The
  spawner captured the request's context lazily, when the batch was first polled on a
  task of its own, so every line a batch logged had no `trace_id` and `current_trace_id()`
  answered `None` there. It now captures on the resolver's task, where async-graphql calls
  it, and runs the batch in the request's span.
- **Breaking:** `nest_rs::seaorm::graphql::LoaderScope`, `nest_rs::graphql::GraphqlBatchContext`,
  `GraphqlBatchSpawner` and `GraphqlBatchFuture` are gone, and so is the boot `warn`
  `dataloaders seeded with no batch context`. A batch carries its request's ability and
  pool by itself: delete `LoaderScope as dyn GraphqlBatchContext` from the GraphQL
  authorization module. A module that still lists it does not compile.
- `nest-rs-database` depends on `nest-rs-core`, whose map holds its executor; it no longer
  depends on `tokio` at runtime, nor does `nest-rs-authz`.
- **An install holds the unit it runs once.** `with_request_scope`, `with_ambient`,
  `with_request_executor`, `with_job_executor`, `with_ability`,
  `RequestContinuation::scope` and `TaskContext::carry` return a future the size of the
  unit plus a few words, where each `async fn` layer held it twice; nested, they reach no
  deeper on the stack than tokio's task-local scopes, where a debug build used to copy
  the unit once per layer. They are awaited as before, and `RequestContinuation::scope`'s
  future no longer borrows the continuation.
- **Changed:** a factory that fails ends the boot naming the type it would have built:
  `FactoryFailedError { type_name, source }`, said as ``the factory for `app::Pool`
  failed: connection refused``. The factory's own error is its source, no longer the
  boot error itself. A configuration refusal is one: a `#[config]` is built by the
  factory `ConfigModule` queues, so `main`'s error opens on ``the factory for
  `features::posts::config::PostsConfig` failed``, and `invalid value for <PREFIX>_…` or
  `configuration validation failed for '…'` follows under `Caused by:`.
- **Added:** `Posture` in `nest_rs::guards::prelude`, beside `HandlerMetadata`.
- **Breaking:** `nest_rs::guards::dispatch::GlobalPoolOperationGuard::{from_container,
  factory}` and `GlobalPoolMcpGuard::{from_container, factory}` take the registered
  bridge (`Option<Arc<dyn GraphqlOperationGuard>>`, `Option<Arc<dyn
  McpOperationGuard>>`) and run it inside the pool.
- **Fixed:** `use_guards_global([])` with no bridge left `/graphql` unguarded
  without a word; the boot now says `graphql operations run unguarded`
  (`mode="unguarded"`), as it does with no pool at all.
- **A failed GitHub verified-email lookup is said at `warn`.** When GitHub's emails
  endpoint fails — an outage, a revoked `user:email` scope — the login goes on with the
  profile email, unverified, as before, and now files one `warn` on `nest_rs::social`
  with `provider = "github"` and the call's error, where it filed a `debug`: a returning
  user on that fallback cannot reach their account.
- **Added:** `nest_rs::http::RouteResponseShaper::GATE`, the class gate a shaper
  type declares (`None` by default), run with the route's guards.
- **Breaking:** `nest_rs::guards::dispatch::wrap_route_shaper` takes the
  route's response-layer stack (`RouteEndpoint`), its class gates and its
  `Handler`; the shaper runs whenever any layer runs at the route, so a
  route-scoped interceptor reads the route's `#[meta]` too.
- **Fixed:** `/graphql` variables that could not be written as JSON reached the
  global pipes as `null`; the operation is now refused with the pipe error
  response ("the operation variables could not be read as JSON: …", the cause
  said without its value) and one `error` on `nest_rs::graphql`.
- **Fixed:** a panic while config bookkeeping was locked no longer turns off
  the contested-variable refusal or lets a `.env` value outrank a pinned
  config. A poisoned lock made the claim check answer `Ok`, the unclaimed
  report forget a variable a reader asked for, and the record of what the
  cascade published answer "not from the cascade", so a committed value read
  as a deployment one; each now recovers the lock, as the rest of the
  framework does. The OAuth client's record of a provider's status and
  `Retry-After` recovers its lock the same way.
- A connection or HTTP/2 stream task that panics is contained by the framework's
  one containment, with the same two lines.
- `connection cap no longer reached; accepting again` is filed only once the
  listener itself waits, never while a failed `accept()` is backed off.
- **Changed:** a boot error naming a dependency or a provider keeps its type
  arguments — `Store<Session>` where it said `Store` — so two instances of one
  generic resource are told apart.
- **Fixed:** in every app importing `HealthModule`, no singleton was ever dropped:
  `HealthService` held a strong clone of the container that holds it. It now hands the
  container back at its module's close.
- **Fixed:** a listener that injects the `EventBus` (one that emits) and the bus kept
  each other alive. The bus lets go of its listeners at `EventsModule`'s close.
- **Fixed: The multi-tenancy page no longer advises a per-connection setting.**
  *Multi-tenant SaaS in production* told you to key a Postgres Row-Level
  Security policy on a setting made per connection. The framework sets no
  database session or transaction setting, and such a setting outlives its
  request on a pooled connection and serves the next tenant. The page now
  states the gap: no database-side backstop exists in 7.0, and closing it
  takes the framework setting the tenant inside every transaction it opens.
  Its processor example now reaches the data through its service and `Repo`,
  as the demo's notifications slice does.
- **Fixed: The test harness stays out of release binaries.**
  `nestrs new` enabled `nest-rs`'s `testing` feature on the workspace's
  `[workspace.dependencies]` line, so every app's release binary linked the
  test harness: since the `queue` forward above, tokio's `test-util` and
  `rcgen` with it. The workspace's `nest-rs` now carries the capabilities
  alone, and each scaffolded app enables `testing` on its own `[dev-
  dependencies]` line, `nest-rs = { workspace = true, features = ["testing"]
  }`. The demo does the same, its features crate forwarding `nest-rs/testing`
  from `test-support`. The tutorial pages show the split.
- **Fixed:** the `#[force_guards]` docs example ran its guard once (see above).
- **Changed:** the HTTP transport's `transport listening` line is filed at `debug`, once
  the socket is bound, with the address it really bound, port `0` resolved; it was an
  `info` filed before the bind, with the configured address. `app ready` carries the
  address at `info`.
- **Fixed:** a seeded config is validated and bounded like a configured one.
  `App::builder().provide(cfg)` was the one path into a `#[config]` that reached
  no check, so a seed past a duration's ceiling or failing `validate()` booted
  and reached whatever read it. Before any factory reads it, a seed — or an
  override — of a config now passes every bound and `validate()` its read
  would, and fails the boot with the same `ConfigError`, naming the field and
  its range or the namespace. The value is kept exactly as seeded; the
  environment is not read. `Config` gains the provided method `check`, the
  checks without a source. A container built by hand (`Container::builder()`)
  drains no factory and checks no seed.
- **Fixed:** the unclaimed-variable report warned on a named instance's
  variable under its vendor's namespace — `<PREFIX>_REDIS__CACHE__CONNECT_TIMEOUT_SECS`
  as a near miss of `<PREFIX>_REDIS__CONNECT_TIMEOUT_SECS` — when the default
  instance was read first. A named instance's namespace is now claimed when its
  module registers it, before any read; a vendor no instance opens still has
  its near misses reported.
- **One denial files one line.** A refusal by `AuthnGuard`, `AbilityGuard` or
  `ThrottlerGuard` filed the guard's own `warn` and the edge's `guard denied …` on
  `nest_rs::layers`, two lines for one denial. The edge now files each guard denial once,
  on the target the guard declares, and no guard files its own
  ([A guard writes its decision once](#a-guard-writes-its-decision-once-derives-its-layer-and-the-edge-files-its-denials)).
- **Fixed: a cut HTTP/1.1 connection no longer holds the way down past its stop bound.**
  A connection runs its handler inside its own task, and the transport waited for every
  cut connection without a bound, so a handler whose drop blocked its thread held `serve`
  as long as it blocked. The cut connections share the half-second settle the HTTP/2
  streams and the self-mounts' work already share, and what still runs then is left to end
  on its own and named at `warn` on `nest_rs::http`: `HTTP connection tasks still ran after
  they were cut; they are left to end on their own` (`connections`).
- **`object_store::registry::DefaultObjectStoreRegistry` is disallowed** beside
  `parse_url` and `parse_url_opts`: its `resolve` opens a store for an unknown
  URL from every process variable, `AWS_ALLOW_INVALID_CERTIFICATES` among them,
  past `StorageConfig` and `ClientTls`.
- **A panic inside a subscriber's own dispatch** — a field whose `Display`
  panics while a line is written — no longer re-enters the subscriber: the
  panic hook says it in one line on stderr, `nestrs: panicked at …`, and the
  process exits `101`. A subscriber holding a lock while it writes, which the
  hook's own event deadlocked on, is safe.
- **A contained unit that panicked is never polled again**, so one panic is
  one `error` line.
- **The access line counts the bytes of every answer the framework renders
  itself** — a `404`, a `405`, a unit whose commit its database refused, a
  handler cut at the request's deadline — where it said none.
- **`Server-Timing` never times an in-band refusal**: a `/graphql` operation
  refused inside a `200`, or a `/mcp` call its guards refused, marks its
  request, which goes out untimed; so does an event stream, whose head leaves
  before the work it streams.
- **Fixed:** the docs gave the shutdown phases NestJS's names without saying they keep
  nestrs's timing. `#[on_module_destroy]`, `#[before_application_shutdown]` and
  `#[on_application_shutdown]` run once every transport has returned from `serve`, never
  at the signal, so no request, job or socket message its transport stopped in time
  meets a provider torn down under it; a transport still serving past its stop bound is
  abandoned there, named at `error`, and the hooks start anyway. Work due at the signal
  waits on `AppLifecycle::stopping()`, and the stop's cause is `AppLifecycle::cause()`.
  The lifecycle page, `LifecyclePhase`'s rustdoc and the decorator table say so (the
  table said `#[before_application_shutdown]` ran before the shutdown began), the NestJS
  comparison gains the rows, and two tests in `nest-rs-core`'s lifecycle suite hold the
  order, after a transport that returned and after one abandoned.
- **Fixed:** the docs say on every client TLS page — Redis, storage, the JWK Set, the
  OAuth client, OpenTelemetry — and on the configuration page's `_FILE` paragraph that
  client TLS material and every `<KEY>_FILE` value are read at the start: a renewed file
  takes effect at the next one, so a rotation is a rolling restart. The HTTP server's own
  certificate renews in place, as before. Postgres reads the certificate files its URL
  names (`sslrootcert`, `sslcert`, `sslkey`) at each connection the pool opens, so a
  renewed one reaches every connection opened after it. Reloading the rest is 7.x's.
- **Fixed: A rollback met by a statement Postgres cancelled is said at `warn`.**
  A unit cut at its deadline leaves its statement running; Postgres cancels it
  at the statement bound (`57014`), and that answer is what the unit's
  rollback then meets. SeaORM filed it as `ERROR transaction rollback failed`,
  seconds after the client was answered, though nothing the transaction wrote
  stood. It is now `WARN transaction rolled back after a statement Postgres
  cancelled; nothing it wrote stands`, with `outcome = "rollback"`, on every
  edge, as every `57014` is said; any other failed rollback stays at `error`.
- **Added:** `nest_rs_codegen::Refusals`, the accumulator `#[module]`'s
  provider list, `#[processor]` and `#[scheduled]` report their refusals
  through.
- **Added:** `nest_rs::core::DuplicateMetaError`, `#[non_exhaustive]`.
- **The demo gives a feature a store of its own on a named instance.** The
  audio feature files a record of each finished transcode on a `Transcodes`
  Redis instance (`<PREFIX>_REDIS__TRANSCODES__URL`: database 0 beside the
  queue in development, database 8 under the test profile, so the e2e suites
  never write into a running app's records), and its progress stream reads it
  after one look at the
  store, rather than asking S3 on every poll; `/health/ready` on the api,
  the worker and the assistant reads `redis:transcodes` beside `redis`,
  `seaorm` and `storage`. The chart's secrets gain `REDIS__TRANSCODES__URL`.
- **Fixed: The outbox's boot refusal names a recipe that exists.**
  An app binding SeaORM and a queue backend over a database without
  `nestrs_outbox` refused to boot telling you to run `nestrs run db migrate`,
  a recipe no workspace has. It now reads: `the outbox table \`nestrs_outbox\`
  is missing: run \`nestrs g migration nestrs_outbox\`, then \`nestrs run db
  up\``.
- **Fixed:** what the `.env` cascade says before any subscriber exists — a file it
  could not read, malformed lines it skipped — reached nobody, since `#[nest_rs::main]`
  publishes the cascade before its runtime and its subscriber. It is now one line on
  stderr each, `nestrs: skipped malformed .env lines path=./.env.local skipped=2`, every
  value escaped, never what the skipped lines held. Any `warn` or `error` a process step
  files there takes the same path; a panic there is still the hook's own
  `nestrs: panicked at …` line.
- **Added: `AccessTokenResponse` states the scope it granted (RFC 6749 §5.1).**
  §5.1 makes `scope` optional in a token response only when it is the scope the
  client requested, and §3.3 says an issuer that grants another one must say so.
  `nest_rs::oauth::server::AccessTokenResponse` had no `scope`, so an issuer that
  narrowed a request, or applied its default to a request naming none, could not
  comply. It gains `scope: Vec<String>`, written as one space-delimited string and
  left out when empty, which reads as "the scope requested"; the OpenAPI document
  publishes it as an optional string. Build the response with
  `AccessTokenResponse::bearer(access_token, expires_in)`, and add
  `.with_scope(granted)` whenever the grant differs from the request:

  ```rust
  Ok(AccessTokenResponse::bearer(access_token, jwt.ttl_secs()).with_scope(granted))
  ```

  A struct literal no longer compiles. The demo's token endpoint now grants the
  scopes a request names within the client's registration, refuses one outside it
  with `invalid_scope`, grants the whole registration when the request names none,
  states the grant, and takes the client's roles from its registered `payload`
  rather than from the scopes it asks for.
- **Added: the 7.x semver policy.** What every 7.x release keeps until 8.0 is written on
  one page, [nestrs.dev/semver](https://nestrs.dev/semver/): the public items outside
  `__private`, the decorator keys, the variables and `#[config]` field names, the wire and
  storage formats (every header NestRS reads or writes among them), the observability
  names, the umbrella's features and the `nestrs` commands and flags — never message
  prose. A new refusal is a break: what compiled keeps compiling, and what booted keeps
  booting. A minor adds — a `#[config]` field included, so build a config with
  `..Default::default()` — and may raise the minimum Rust version, named here; a patch
  fixes and never raises it. A package depends on
  `nest-rs = { version = "7.N", default-features = false, features = [...] }`, never on
  another `nest-rs-*` crate but `nest-rs-codegen` from a decorator crate. One fix
  is a patch although it refuses what used to boot: a security fix turning away a
  configuration a standard calls insecure, under the changelog's `### Security`,
  with an upgrading note naming the configuration refused and the secure one.
- **Breaking: an MCP rate-limit bucket is keyed on the operation's name, without its kind.**
  A tool and a prompt sharing a name share one budget, and the buckets a shared store held
  under the old key expire within their window.

[Unreleased]: https://github.com/YV17labs/NestRS/compare/v6.1.0...HEAD
